   The constants never depended on how many arms were selected or on their individual con-
tinuation lengths. A shorter actual arm contributes only its actual points; it was not extended
through an unvisited disk. The same argument covers limiting visited tangencies because all
integrated points have positive visited incidences. It controls their interior source approach, not
an arbitrarily prescribed trace measure supported on the singular boundary. General Borel cuts
remain measurable selectors throughout; their pointwise or angular boundary limits are not
assumed to exist. The assertion for unions follows from one-copy positive domination, not from
adding separately normalized probabilities.                                                      □
28.2. What has and has not been transferred to the original roof. Theorems 28.2 and
28.5 remove the need for Taylor remainders and tangent sectors for the stated synchronized
coarea and physical-band budgets. In    √ particular (28.9) is a collision-uniform O(s) statement
and (28.11) is a collision-uniform O( s) statement on the full defined arm family. These are
continuum estimates, not conclusions of a finite search or a fitted exponent.
   They do not close (25.3). In the actual roof density at a common t, formula (28.6) must be
evaluated at the branch-dependent radius rx (t, θ). The synchronized sum (28.7) evaluates all
branches at the same r and is not a bound for that different sum. Likewise L − a(θ) is used only
to specify a physical subset; it is not substituted for L in the exact record, Fourier reference,
conditioning or LLT.
   The band budget bounds the original collision-source measure. The paired measure cε du dθ
uses the minimum of its two endpoint traces and is not automatically a submeasure of the
interior density f ε du dθ. Its transfer still requires the retained positive-variation/comparison
argument. The inverse-gradient estimate does not include the unbounded insertion Im , and no
such insertion is silently absorbed into its constant. Critical-origin arms do not exhaust the
positive-radius singular cuts, noncritical folds or low-load source. These sources, the first-order
clearance contribution, nongrazing capacity, and the original spectral, arithmetic and conditional-
denominator interfaces retain their exact proof obligations. No factor m2 is attached to the new
unscaled bounds at a fixed outer width and declared harmless. The original ordered limit and
four-coordinate raw local inversion target remain unchanged and are not asserted to be proved
here.

        29. Grazing-uniform endpoint localization and the third variation
   The estimates in this section are on every actual regular finite itinerary, not on an auxiliary
stochastic process. They hold for all m ≥ 1 and R ∈ [9/20, 47/100], without a lower cutoff
on any positive incidence. The derivative is with respect to the two endpoint angles of the
actual stationary action. It is not the derivative of the forward collision map with fixed initial
momentum. This distinction is essential: no uniform bound on that expanding forward derivative
is asserted.

Remark 29.4 (Domain, time, and source). Theorem 29.3 concerns the true reduced action at
every regular point. It requires neither a pressure theorem nor the endpoint-momentum kernel.
Uniform boundedness of its third derivative is not uniform control of all higher derivatives, a
chart-count bound, or a roof-density theorem. All labels may restrict the points at which it is
used, but are not differentiated. For a path of genuine trajectories with a fixed itinerary reaching
a physical boundary the estimates hold up to that boundary. They do not define a collision-map
derivative at a visited tangency or permit continuing an exact event through a new hit.

       31. A grazing-uniform resolvent for the physical reflection equations
   We retain the actual circular-scatterer trajectories, radius interval, unit-speed clock, and all
exact selectors of (1.1). The issue here is the derivative of an endpoint boundary-value problem,
not a derivative bound for the expanding forward collision map. All derivatives are taken at
regular physical points of a fixed actual itinerary. No unvisited disk is removed from its admissible
domain.
   Put a∗ = R+ /τ− = 47/6, λ = 50/47, and I = {1, . . . , m − 1}. A sum over I is empty when
m = 1. Write Xi = xi+1 − xi , ϑi = arg Xi , using local real lifts of these angles. The specular law
is equivalent, on the physical branch, to constancy of
                            Ei (α) = 2αi − ϑi−1 (α) − ϑi (α),     i ∈ I.                      (31.1)
Its constant is an odd multiple of π. The value of that multiple does not enter a derivative; it is
fixed on each local lift. The reflection equation is not obtained by assuming that division by a
small incidence is harmless.

Proof. The off-diagonal entries of A are nonpositive. Let Djj = 2 + Rcj (wj−1 + wj ) and write

  Each column of A has sum at least 2: 1T A ≥ 21T . Multiplication on the right by the

argument uses column, not row, dominance. Row dominance can fail when one incidence is much

Corollary 31.5 (Fixed-itinerary grazing limits, without a fictitious event). Fix m and a sequence
of actual regular trajectories with the same centers and convergent collision angles and radius.
Suppose the limit is in the original radius interval. Any number of interior incidences may tend
to zero. The local reflection equations at the limiting configuration have a unique smooth formal
solution in the endpoint angles; every finite endpoint jet of the original physical branch has that
formal limit. The bounds in (31.18) persist. This does not make the formal continuation an
admissible billiard trajectory beyond its actual first boundary.
Proof. Distinct successive disk centers and R ≤ R+ keep every limiting flight length at least τ− .
Local direction logarithms are smooth there. The limiting Jacobian is invertible by Theorem 31.2,
including simultaneous zero incidences. The implicit function theorem at that configuration
supplies the formal reflection solution, with the radius also treated as a smooth parameter. Local
uniqueness identifies every sufficiently close member of the given physical sequence with it. Its
finite jets converge, and their uniform bounds pass to the limit. An extra disk can obstruct the
formal solution, and the prescribed section or return event can end at the boundary. Neither
obstruction is discarded; no collision-map derivative at tangency is asserted.                  □

       32. Fourth physical variation and the exact high-order cancellation
   The third-variation result of Section 29 is retained. It does not by itself bound a fourth
derivative: differentiating the stationary lift introduces its second derivative. The reflection
resolvent now controls that derivative without a small-incidence loss. The following proof includes
those terms rather than continuing the third-order Schur cancellation beyond its valid scope.

32.1. What this resolves in the original source problem. The higher reflection jets and
(32.5) remove an actual local analytic obstruction: repeated stationary differentiation need not
incur powers of the smallest visited incidence or an uncontrolled factor equal to the collision
count. They also justify the displayed higher-order Taylor errors on every existing physical chart.
These are uniform statements about all actual regular itineraries, not assertions from a finite
sample or a replacement Markov model.
   They do not count physical charts or continue through a newly hit disk. The least unvisited
clearance can still cut a chart, the original section/return selector can still jump, and a positive
one-sided paired trace is not automatically a bulk-collision submeasure. In particular no norm of
the algebraic reflection resolvent is a pressure estimate for the twisted transfer operator.
   The remaining target is still (15.8), with B → ∞ outermost and the full one-copy physical
sum made before the original radius, exact-label and roof suprema and collision limsup. Neither
(31.18) nor (32.4) supplies the missing m−2 common-physical-roof summation or nongrazing
capacity. The two averaged clauses of Input 3.1, the stronger pressure/pairing interfaces and the
original exact arithmetic and conditioned-denominator hypotheses retain their distinct status.
No theorem or target is deleted to hide these remaining obligations.

        33. Uniform analytic lifts and genuine physical continuation tubes
   All radii, disks, collision labels, physical clocks and the original section are unchanged.
The constants in this section are deliberately conservative; their purpose is a positive radius
independent of the number of actual collisions. Unlike a bound for each fixed derivative, the first
theorem provides one common domain of analytic continuation. A separate geometric argument
determines when this continuation is a true billiard trajectory. The latter step is not inferred
from analyticity.

No physical passage through grazing is asserted.

part is at most r∗ /16. Its Lipschitz constant is at most Kr∗ ≤ 1/8. The successive iterates from

                                                               ′
                      c0i ≥ εrdi   (0 ≤ i ≤ m),       ∆0i ≥ εrdi   (0 ≤ i < m).                  (33.4)

                                                                  ′
                                ci (h) ≥ 21 εrdi , ∆i (h) ≥ 12 εrdi .                        (33.5)
The physical clock is the sum of the true lengths. The original section, visit count and terminal
test are evaluated at the resulting physical orbit; they are not declared constant on the tube.
Thus any Borel exact-record selector can be retained pointwise without adding a section-distance
condition to the guard.
Proof. The norm bound in (33.3) gives |αi (h) − αi0 | ≤ P∗ sλ−di . For real angles the corresponding
endpoint displacement is at most R+ P∗ sλ−di . The Hausdorff distance between the two closed
                                    ′
flight segments is at most Cclr sλ−di . Distance to every third disk is one-Lipschitz for this distance;
taking the infimum over all centers except exactly the two incident centers preserves it. This
controls every unvisited obstacle at once, including switches of the minimizing obstacle or closest
segment point.
    For a nonzero real flight, comparison of normalized vectors gives |X/|X| − X 0 /|X 0 || ≤
2|X − X 0 |/|X 0 |. Since adjacent depth weights have ratio at most λ, each signed departure
normal component, and each negative arrival normal component, changes by at most Cinc sλ−di .
The same bound holds for the signed tangential component. Initially both physical normal
components at an interior reflection equal c0i . The exact rational inequality
                                                    504

                                                   473 53
implies λ−d ≤ rd . The choice of δ∗ therefore proves both half-margins, first for signed normal
components and segment clearances of the formal real lift.
   Their strict positivity makes that lift physical. A positive outgoing normal component keeps
a segment outside its initial disk; a negative incoming normal component keeps it outside its
final disk until the stated endpoint. All other disks have strictly positive clearance. Thus each
segment is the true next free flight, not a flight with a hidden earlier collision. The fixed reflection
equations, together with these normal signs, give specular reflection at every interior endpoint.
Concatenation proves the actual ordered word and unchanged displacement, with no deleted
collision. Its unit-speed duration is its geometric length. Section membership can jump as its
boundary is crossed, so the original selector is retained rather than differentiated or frozen. This
makes no assertion about the source outside the tube.                                                  □
   The guard margins in (33.4) already occur in the original product (1.2); they are not a new
conditioning event for the target probability. The theorem establishes an explicit domain property
of that existing geometric class. Points failing the margins, and all one-sided singular traces,
remain present in the full source problem. No claim that their normalized mass tends to zero is
made by constructing these tubes.

an explicit comparison mechanism on the uniform physical tubes just constructed. It uses
logarithmic optical distortion before summation, not an exchange of the roof supremum and the
sum. The only global root input is the retained physical kernel Theorem 26.4, with its native
growth dependencies left unchanged.

Lemma 34.2 (A common physical momentum disk at every guarded critical root). For every
x ∈ Zε,m,R there is an actual regular inverse qx (ζ) for |ζ| < ρε , where ζ = (p0 , pm ) and qx (0) is
the original endpoint pair. It is contained in the physical endpoint ball |q − qx (0)|2 < sε and obeys
                        |qx (ζ) − qx (0)|2 ≤ R+ |ζ|/g,    ux (ζ) ≤ Cdist ux (0).
This is a common domain for all collision words in the stated class, not a separate radius chosen
after m is fixed.
Proof. The Euclidean ball of radius sε lies strictly inside the ℓ1 endpoint tube of Theorem 33.3.
At a critical center both endpoint tangential components vanish. The signed tangential estimate
in that proof and Cinc δ∗ ≤ 1/2 imply |p0 |, |pm | ≤ ε/2 on the entire ball; in particular c0 , cm ≥ 1/2
and G ⪰ gI there. The domain and every segment used next are genuine physical domains,
established before using the Hessian.
   Given |ζ| < ρε , minimize F (q) − R(Dζ) · (q − qx (0)) on the closed endpoint ball, where
D = diag(−1, 1). On its boundary the radial first variation is at least gs2ε − R+ |ζ|sε > 0. A
boundary minimizer is impossible, because moving radially inward would strictly decrease the
function. Strong convexity gives one interior minimizer, satisfying ∇F = RDζ, and hence the
correct endpoint momenta. The ordinary inverse theorem gives its smooth dependence, while
strong convexity gives g|q − qx (0)|2 ≤ R+ |ζ|. The optical mixed entry is nonzero, so this is also
a regular local parametrization of the original collision source. Lemma 34.1, integrated on the
physical radial segment in endpoint space, gives the stated relative bound. In it ∥q −qx (0)∥∞ ≤ sε
and sε /ε = δ∗ /2.                                                                                    □

34.1. The remaining normalized source is not eliminated. The new analytic domain,
physical guard tube and common-roof summation remove two specific gaps of the earlier critical
argument: no collision-dependent continuation radius is chosen, and different branch momentum
radii are now compared by a proved multiplicative optical bound. They neither assert that all
critical centers satisfy a common guard margin nor estimate every original singular, noncritical
or paired-boundary source. The latter measures need not be restrictions of the bulk collision
measure used here.
   Even on the covered union, (34.4) is an O(1) roof-height bound, not an O(m−2 ) bound. It
cannot be multiplied by m2 and declared negligible. The envelope (34.5) isolates genuine physical
roof locations but provides no unproved critical-roof nonconcentration rate or transfer of return
labels across a section cut. The missing original limit remains exactly (15.8), not a renamed
substitute. To close it requires a normalized complete physical-source estimate or sufficient
                         COLLISION RECORDS AND RAW LOCAL INVERSION                                89

original nongrazing capacity, including the uncovered incidence-only low-load source, unvisited
and visited boundary terms, and the retained spectral, arithmetic and conditioned-denominator
interfaces. Its original widths stay fixed before m → ∞ and B → ∞ remains outermost. No
limit has been interchanged in the new finite-width, all-collision theorem.

     36. Normal-boundary continuation and transversality of the physical clock
   A regular critical record is the actual m-flight record of Section 17 with p0 = pm = 0. It is one
half of a true reversing periodic trajectory. We vary the common physical disk radius within
[R− , R+ ], keeping the ordered lattice centers O0 , . . . , Om , all m collisions and the unit-speed
clock. A formal reflection continuation will always be distinguished from a physical continuation.
The latter is required to avoid every third disk and preserve the actual first-hit order.
36.1. The full normal-boundary reflection equations. Choose local real lifts of the contact
angles αi and of the flight directions ϑi . Their normal-reflection equations are, with the constants
fixed at the reference record,
             E0 = α0 − ϑ0 ,     Ei = 2αi − ϑi−1 − ϑi (1 ≤ i < m),                   Em = αm − ϑm−1 .        (36.1)
At a physical critical record E0 is zero modulo 2π and Em is π modulo 2π. The interior constants
are the appropriate fixed odd multiples of π. These lifts remove any branch-cut ambiguity in the
following derivatives.

Remark 36.5 (Fixed-radius shortage is not a radius average). The lower clock slope is a new
all-word geometric input on the original radius family. Formula (36.12) is an auxiliary exact
change of variables, not a replacement of the original probability problem by a radius-averaged
limit theorem. A bound for crossings summed at different radii does not follow from the
retained momentum kernel at one radius; conversely, a small radius integral cannot control supR .
Neither (36.11) nor the presence of moderately transverse collisions proves the original m−2
roof nonconcentration, the full nongrazing comparison capacity, or the shortage limit (15.8).
Singular/paired endpoint traces, nonguarded critical centers and the complete noncritical positive
source remain part of that limit. All original microscopic widths remain fixed before the collision
limsup and B remains outermost. No theorem or proof body of the inherited raw-LLT and
inverse program has been removed.

     37. The complete record: endpoint work and radius–roof disintegration
  The preceding clock calculation extends beyond reversing records, but its endpoint work must
then be retained. This section treats every regular physical m-flight record, with all original
exact labels, not just the critical tubes of Section 34. It produces a transverse coordinate for
                        COLLISION RECORDS AND RAW LOCAL INVERSION                               95

a parameter-extended source. It does not change the fixed-radius probability space or the raw
target.
Theorem 37.1 (Uniform continuation at prescribed endpoint momenta). Fix any actual regular
record and its two endpoint momenta (a, v) = (p0 , pm ) ∈ (−1, 1)2 . Keep them fixed while varying
the common radius. Replace the endpoint constants in (36.1) by
                E0 = − arcsin a (mod 2π),        Em = π + arcsin v (mod 2π).
                                                          √                   √
The full derivative matrix is still (36.2), now with c0 = 1 − a2 and cm = 1 − v 2 in place
of one. All three bounds (36.3) remain valid, as do the common formal radius disk (36.4) and
the physical radius response (36.6). None of their constants depends on (a, v), the word, or
the smallest incidence. Physicality is asserted only on connected regular physical continuation
intervals, not on the whole formal radius disk.
Proof. The departure direction is α0 +arcsin a. The incoming terminal direction is αm +π−arcsin v;
tangential momentum is unchanged by the last specular reflection. These give exactly the two
displayed constants modulo 2π. Their derivatives with respect to R vanish because a, v are fixed.
The flight differentiation in Theorem 36.1 then gives the same matrix with its true endpoint
cosines. Its column excess remains exactly bi , including the endpoint columns: each endpoint
cosine appears both in its diagonal correction and in the sole off-diagonal entry of that column.
The unweighted and weighted column arguments therefore apply without alteration, and the row
bound follows by the same geometric sum. No derivative of arcsin a or arcsin v is taken. The
complex flight estimates in Theorem 36.2 use only the positive flight lengths, the disk radius
bound and this inverse, not normality. They supply the same formal continuation and the same
bound on α′ . The interpretation of its physical subinterval is unchanged.                     □
Theorem 37.2 (The physical roof has only bounded endpoint work). Let L(R) be the true
physical roof on any connected physical continuation interval from Theorem 37.1. Then

In particular for all m ≥ m0 , the actual roof is strictly monotone on each connected physical
component, and −L′ (R) ≥ κ0 m/2. These statements are uniform over the entire original radius
interval, over the endpoint momenta and over all physical collision words. They impose no new
incidence or clearance guard on the original source. The conservative constant m0 is a theoretical
threshold, not an experimentally reached collision count.
Proof. On the stationary interior of the full action, its endpoint gradient is R(−a, v). Differ-
entiating the action along the prescribed-momentum continuation consequently leaves the two
endpoint chain-rule terms, in addition to the explicit radius derivative. The latter telescopes as
in (36.7), proving (37.1). Its endpoint work has absolute value at most R(|a| + |v|)DR ≤ Cend .
The visited incidence sum is bounded below by γ0 ⌊m/94⌋ from Corollary 35.3. Dropping only
the two nonnegative endpoint cosines in a lower bound, and using ⌊m/94⌋ ≥ m/94 − 1, gives
                          −L′ (R) ≥ 2γ0 ⌊m/94⌋ − Cend ≥ κ0 m − Closs .

There are exactly 2m incidence contributions with multiplicity, each at most one, which gives
the upper bound. The definition of m0 proves the last assertion. This is a pointwise geometric
estimate for every physical record, not an ergodic average. In particular no independence, pressure
input or collision-word count occurs in its proof.                                               □

Proof. On a regular physical branch the initial collision measure is dα0 da/(4π) and the map to
(a, v) has positive absolute Jacobian |∂α0 pm |; this is the actual root Jacobian used in the physical

cover the regular source: use countably many lattice words and second countability in (R, α0 , a).
A fixed first-chart allocation makes their representations disjoint. The allocation indicators and
any section or exact-label cuts stay inside h; none is differentiated. Applying substitution to
finite positive chart sums and then monotone convergence gives the equality for the complete
regular source, allowing infinite values. The identity concerns the regular source and makes no
claim about the separate paired-boundary traces. Finally (37.2) proves (37.4).                      □
Remark 37.4 (The precise unpaid step is unchanged). This exact formula retains all regular
physical records, all original collision labels and their true physical roofs. It is not a new
probability model and does not discard low-incidence records. Nevertheless its crossing sum is
taken at different radii. The unscaled root-kernel bound at a common (R, a, v) does not control
this sum. Nor does an estimate integrated in R control the original fixed-radius roof essential
height. A uniform bound for those concentrations, the complete positive source and paired
                              COLLISION RECORDS AND RAW LOCAL INVERSION                                             97

traces, or the original comparison capacity is still required to prove (15.8). This remains a
substantive mathematical obligation rather than an automatic consequence of the factor 1/m
in a different-coordinate Jacobian. All original probability quantifiers and ordered microscopic
limits are retained.

           38. A global variational certificate for every physical word
   This section supplies a comparison valid on the whole original physical source, without requiring
a common clearance margin or a physical critical center. It extends, rather than reclaims as new,
the Cartesian support argument of Lemma 10.1 and the fixed-endpoint minimizing property in
Lemma 10.3. The new ingredients are a complete disk-word duality formula, an exact endpoint
defect, and quantitative certificates with nonnegative terms.
   Fix m ≥ 1, R ∈ [R− , R+ ], and an ordered lifted center word w = (O0 , . . . , Om ), with O0 = 0
and Oi+1 ̸= Oi . A word of an actual trajectory always satisfies this last condition. Set

minimization. An arbitrary minimizer is not called a Lorentz trajectory: it may have an interior
vertex, a tangency, or a segment intersecting an unlisted disk. Neither ℓw nor the minimization
defines a replacement dynamics, a roof, a probability law, or a new conditioning event. The
actual clock throughout remains L = S(Q) on the original first-hit trajectory Q. Successive disks
are separated by at least 1 − 2R+ = 3/50, so S is continuously differentiable on a neighborhood
of Kw (R).

            39. Global radius order and a positive physical shape energy
   The radius–roof identities of Sections 36 and 37 are inherited. Here a global variational value
allows critical comparisons between disconnected physical continuation intervals, and its second
variation has a nonnegative, geometrically explicit form. Nothing is continued as a true trajectory
through a forbidden collision or an unvisited disk.
Theorem 39.1 (Global radius convexity of the disk-word value). For each fixed word of Section 38,
R 7→ ℓw (R) is convex and strictly decreasing on [R− , R+ ]. For R1 < R2 ,
                          2(R2 − R1 ) ≤ ℓw (R1 ) − ℓw (R2 ) ≤ 2m(R2 − R1 ).                    (39.1)
This statement concerns the geometric value even where its minimizer is not physical. If regular
physical critical records of the same word exist at both R1 , R2 , with true roofs a1 , a2 , then the
stronger bound
                  κ0 m(R2 − R1 ) ≤ a1 − a2 ≤ 2m(R2 − R1 ),            κ0 = 1/210560,           (39.2)
holds without requiring a physical continuation on (R1 , R2 ). Thus a fixed word has at most one
regular physical critical record with a prescribed physical roof over the whole radius interval.
Proof. The dual formula (38.2) is a maximum of affine functions Cw (V ) − RT (V ) over the same
compact vector set for all R. It proves convexity. At each Rj , choose the optimal unit-edge
vector Vj furnished by the primal minimizer. Its affine function supports ℓw from below and
2 ≤ T (Vj ) ≤ 2m. Therefore
                      T (V2 )(R2 − R1 ) ≤ ℓw (R1 ) − ℓw (R2 ) ≤ T (V1 )(R2 − R1 ).
This proves (39.1), without differentiability of ℓw or a common minimizing configuration.
   At a regular physical critical point, Corollary 38.3 identifies its true roof with ℓw . Its actual
velocity vector is an optimal dual vector and T (V ) = 2 + 2 m−1
                                                              i=1 ci ≥ κ0 m by the already proved

complete-bending bound (36.8). Use that vector at R2 in the lower supporting inequality and

fixed radius the critical point is unique by the inherited physical uniqueness theorem; distinct
radii have strictly ordered roofs by the new global comparison. No physical orbit in a hole has
been asserted or counted.                                                                          □

      40. Physical clearance certificates and complete-source localization
  The endpoint defect in (38.5) is available for every original regular trajectory, including those
whose geometric word minimum is not a true critical orbit. We quantify this distinction rather
than discarding that source. The final proposition then inserts the proved enclosure into the
existing exact source formula, at the original fixed radius and roof.
Theorem 40.1 (A quantitative certificate relative to every minimizing contact). Let Q be an
arbitrary true regular record and let E = ER (Q). For every minimizer Y defining ℓw (R) set
                                   √               q
                      δ0 = δm = 2 2RE,        δi = RE/ci (0 < i < m).
Then |Yi − Qi | ≤ δi for every i. Consequently, for every unvisited center z on every original
flight, without a lattice truncation,
                  dist(z, [Yi , Yi+1 ]) − R ≥ dist(z, [Qi , Qi+1 ]) − R − max(δi , δi+1 ).      (40.1)
There is a sufficient proved physicality certificate: if
                             ∆i > max(δi , δi+1 )    (0 ≤ i < m),                               (40.2)
                              ci > (δi−1 + 2δi + δi+1 )/τ−      (0 < i < m),                    (40.3)
then every minimizer Y is the same regular physical normal-endpoint record of the original word.
No section label is thereby transferred from Q to Y . If these tests fail, Q stays in the original
source; its minimum is not declared physical.
102                                             QIAN QI

Proof. Insert the actual velocity vector of Q and a minimizing Y into (38.3). Weak duality and
(38.4) give 0 ≤ S(Y ) − Dw,R (V ) ≤ E. Thus each nonnegative contact term is at most E. At an
interior contact it equals 2ci {R − ni · (Yi − Oi )} ≥ (ci /R)|Yi − Qi |2 , which proves the interior
bound.
   At departure the supporting disk point for −v0 is O0 + Rv0 ; the endpoint contact slack bounds
                                                                    2         2
its squared distance from Y0 by 2RE.    √ Also |Q0 − (O0 + Rv0 )| = 2R (1 − c0 ) ≤ 2RE. The
triangle inequality gives |Y0 − Q0 | ≤ 2 2RE. At arrival use Om − Rvm−1 and the same argument.
For a common affine interpolation parameter the distance between two corresponding segment
points is at most max(δi , δi+1 ). Both directed Hausdorff distances satisfy this bound. Distance
to any fixed z is 1-Lipschitz for Hausdorff distance; this proves (40.1) for all lattice centers
simultaneously.
   It remains to justify physicality, not merely small displacement. The edges of Y have length
at least τ− = 3/50, because successive original disks are disjoint. If vbi is their unit direction, the
normalization inequality |u/|u| − v/|v|| ≤ 2|u − v|/ min(|u|, |v|) gives
                                     |vbi − vi | ≤ 2(δi + δi+1 )/τ− .
Hence the ith interior gradient of S at Y has norm at least 2ci − 2(δi−1 + 2δi + δi+1 )/τ− > 0. By
the minimizing condition in Theorem 38.1, Yi lies on its circle and that gradient is a negative
multiple of the outward normal. Equality of the two adjacent unit speeds then makes the
incoming normal component strictly negative and the outgoing one strictly positive, with equal
tangential components: this is exactly the regular exterior specular law. At the endpoints the
gradients have norm one and give normal departure and arrival. The strict third-disk inequalities
(40.2) exclude every unlisted collision; the signs just proved exclude another intersection with
either incident disk inside its flight. Thus Y is a true regular first-hit record. Corollary 38.3
gives uniqueness. Nothing in the proof keeps section visits constant along a competitor or along
a homotopy; the original labels of Q have not changed.                                           □
Proposition 40.2 (A deterministic positive junction cost). Partition a word into consecutive
nonempty blocks at any chosen indices. For each block choose a minimizing unit-edge dual vector
as in Theorem 38.1. Denote the last vector before a junction by v − and the first after it by v + .
Then
                                                        (2 − |v − − v + |).
                                 X                 X
                       ℓw (R) ≥       ℓb (R) + R                                           (40.4)
                                   blocks b           junctions
Every junction term is nonnegative. If both block minima at a junction are physical critical
records with terminal/departure normals n− , n+ , the corresponding term is R(2 − |n− + n+ |).
The comparison does not assert physical realizability of any block minimum and does not factor
any trajectory probability.
Proof. Concatenate the chosen dual flight vectors. They form a feasible dual vector for the
whole word. The center term Cw is the sum of the block center terms. At a junction the two
separate endpoint penalties are R|v − | + R|v + | = 2R, whereas the penalty of the concatenated
dual vector is R|v − − v + |. All other penalties agree. Substitution into (38.2) proves (40.4),
including each junction once. In the physical critical case v − = −n− and v + = n+ , giving the
asserted normal expression. This argument is an exact vector comparison, not a renewal or
Markov decomposition.                                                                         □
Proposition 40.3 (Word localization of the unchanged complete roof source). Fix the original
m, R, n, k and any nonnegative Borel trajectory weight h. Let xw (t, ϑ) be exactly the partial
physical inverse in (10.6), with all original event cuts. That formula admits the additional exact
mask
                                    1{ℓw (R)≤t≤ℓw (R)+ER (xw (t,ϑ))}                        (40.5)
                         COLLISION RECORDS AND RAW LOCAL INVERSION                               103

in every existing summand, without changing either side. In particular only words with ℓw (R) ∈
[t − 2R, t] can contribute. This assertion requires no physical critical center, clearance guard,
pressure hypothesis or incidence lower bound for the regular source.

40.1. The original positive shortage is still the closing condition. The new results remove
the need for a physical critical anchor in the geometric word enclosure; certify an actual-versus-
unphysical minimizing configuration using nonnegative gaps; give a positive junction defect; and
compare critical roofs across disconnected physical radius domains. They do not identify the
auxiliary ℓw with a physical clock when the certificate fails. In particular (40.5) does not replace
the observed microscopic window by [t − 2R, t]. The observed quantity is still the density at the
original t with the original exact labels; the interval is solely an upper-support enclosure in a
separate deterministic word statistic.
   No new theorem here supplies a normalized roof-height estimate for the remaining positive
source or enough nongrazing comparison capacity. The raw target is still (1.5), with the very
same minimal ordered closure condition (15.8). It includes the low-load incidence-only source,
first-order unvisited clearance, noncritical singular charts, one-sided paired/section traces and all
original arithmetic/denominator requirements. The finite source sum is taken before supR ess supt
and before the collision limsup; all microscopic widths remain fixed until that limsup, and B → ∞
is outermost. Neither an auxiliary convex value, a positive curvature identity, a finite diagnostic,
nor the radius-integrated coarea pays that still unproved limit automatically.

Remark 41.4 (What is new relative to the complete predecessor). The earlier pointwise contact
                          −1/2

fresh probability hypothesis. The result concerns every point of a continuous compact sublevel,
not a list of sampled minima. It neither constructs an orbit through a singularity nor controls
forward-time derivatives at grazing.

               42. All-center clearance and a true physical reference
  For a record Q put Si (Q) = [Qi , Qi+1 ]. Define the true third-disk clearance of a flight by
                Γi (Q) =        inf          dist(z, Si (Q)) − R,         Γ(Q) = min Γi (Q).      (42.1)
                           z∈Λ\{Oi ,Oi+1 }                                       0≤i<m

The infimum includes disks visited elsewhere in the word. It excludes only the two incident centers
of this particular flight. Distances are Euclidean distances to closed segments, so a minimizing
foot may change or reach an endpoint. There is no chosen differentiable nearest-center label.
                          COLLISION RECORDS AND RAW LOCAL INVERSION                                    107

Theorem 42.1 (One modulus for every third lattice disk). For every Y ∈ S(Q) and d(E) =
22E 1/4 ,
                                    dH (Si (Y ), Si (Q)) ≤ d(E),                                    (42.2)
                                                                                    2
                     dist(z, Si (Y )) − dist(z, Si (Q)) ≤ d(E)     for every z ∈ R ,                (42.3)
                                      |Γi (Y ) − Γi (Q)| ≤ d(E).                                    (42.4)
All these estimates hold simultaneously over all flights and all lattice centers. They require no
lower bound on any interior incidence.
Proof. Match points with the same affine segment parameter s ∈ [0, 1]. Their difference is
(1 − s)δi + sδi+1 , whose norm is at most max{|δi |, |δi+1 |} ≤ d(E) by Theorem 41.3. This bounds
both directed Hausdorff distances. For two nonempty compact sets at Hausdorff distance at most
d, the triangle inequality gives dist(z, A) ≤ dist(z, B) + d and the reverse inequality, proving
(42.3). Take infima over the same center set in both directions to obtain (42.4). The infima are
finite. No counting or differentiability of the minimizing center is used.                     □
Theorem 42.2 (A grazing-independent clearance certificate for a physical minimum). Let Q be
any original regular record, and put
                                         q

The first condition is vacuous for m = 1. Then A has exactly one global minimum on K. This
minimum is an actual regular exterior first-hit trajectory of the same ordered collision word, with
normal initial and final endpoints. All these assertions concern the unchanged radius R and
actual circular obstacles. They assert no equality of its section/return labels or physical roof with
those of Q.
Proof. Compactness supplies at least one minimizing Y . Since A(Y ) ≤ A(Q), Theorem 41.3 and
Lemma 41.1 apply. Each edge error obeys
                                                      q
                                      |vbi − vi | ≤    2E/ℓi ≤ ε(E).
All edges are nonzero because consecutive disks are separated. The gradient of A with respect to
an interior vertex is dbi = vbi−1 − vbi . By the physical reflection law of Q and (42.5),
                             |dbi | ≥ 2ci − |ei−1 | − |ei | ≥ 2ci − 2ε(E) > 0.
First-order optimality on the ith closed disk means Yi minimizes its linear functional dbi · z. Hence
Yi = Oi + Rn b i with dbi = −|dbi |n
                                   b i . For two unit vectors a, b with a − b = −λn, λ > 0, taking the
scalar product with a + b shows a · n = −b · n; taking it with n gives a · n = −λ/2 and b · n = λ/2.
Their tangential components are equal. This proves a true nontangent specular reflection at each
interior vertex, with the correct exterior signs. At the endpoints the gradients are −vb0 and vbm−1 ,
of norm one. Their disk minimizers give n                        b m = −vbm−1 , precisely normal endpoints.

   Theorem 42.1 and the strict second inequality in (42.5) give Γi (Y ) > 0 for every flight. Thus
no third disk meets any flight, including a disk appearing at a different time in the word. The
two incident disks are avoided in the open flight as well: positive departing normal component
implies |Yi + svbi − Oi |2 = R2 + 2Rs(n     b i · vbi ) + s2 > R2 for s > 0, and the analogous backward
formula applies at arrival. This proves first-hit physicality without discarding any collision.
   For uniqueness, use this physical Y as the reference in (41.7). Its normal endpoints have
defect zero. Any other minimum Z has A(Z) = A(Y ), so the nonnegative gap is zero. All
108                                             QIAN QI

contact coefficients are strictly positive (one at each endpoint and 2cbi internally), and (41.8)
forces Zi = Yi at every vertex. Thus the minimum is unique.                                    □
Remark 42.3 (Sufficiency, not source deletion or capacity). The test may fail while a physical
minimum still exists. Failed records remain in the original source. The incidence requirement in
(42.5) checks nonzero comparison reflections; it is not an inverse-incidence loss in the clearance
modulus. An actual critical reference is one trajectory, not a positive measure comparison pool.
Exact section visits and return labels must be evaluated anew on it, not transported from Q by
proximity. The original observed roof remains L(Q), never A(Y ).
Proposition 42.4 (Positive inclusion on the unchanged exact-record source). Fix R, m and any
of the original exact return/displacement/collision events Π, with its original section probability
measure νR ∗ and nonnegative measurable weight h. Let B be the subset of regular records whose

ordered disk word does not have a unique regular physical normal-endpoint global minimum. Set
        W = {Q : ∃i ∈ {1, . . . , m − 1}, ci (Q) ≤ ε(E(Q))     or   Γ(Q) ≤ d(E(Q))} .          (42.6)
On the regular source, B ⊆ W. Consequently the following positive measure inequality holds on
the original physical roof axis:
                                            ∗                     ∗
                               L∗ 1B 1Π h dνR       ≤ L∗ 1W 1Π h dνR .                         (42.7)
This includes all physical words with one-copy multiplicity and extends to nonnegative marked
sums by monotone convergence. It changes neither the conditioned event nor any roof observation
window. It asserts no small upper bound on the right-hand side.
Proof. The inclusion is the contrapositive of Theorem 42.2. At fixed R, m the physical ordered-
word collection is countable; the property defining B is a property of a word, not of a chosen
minimizer. Thus B is a union of its measurable regular word cylinders. The functions in (42.6)
are measurable on every regular chart: each center-to-segment distance is continuous, the center
set is countable, and finite minima and the displayed elementary functions preserve measurability.
No measurable selection of a minimum is needed.
   Multiply the pointwise inclusion by 1Π h1{L∈I} for any Borel roof set I, then integrate. This
gives (42.7), initially for bounded h and then by increasing truncation. Apply the same argument
on disjoint original marked copies and use monotone convergence for complete nonnegative sums.
The section normalization is the original one already in νR∗ ; no additional Jacobian or copy factor

appears.                                                                                          □
42.1. Exact remaining analytic obligation. If the two pushforwards are absolutely continuous,
(42.7) orders their densities almost everywhere at the same R, m, n, k, t. Without that hypothesis
it remains a measure inequality, not an assertion that a singular component vanishes. Paired
boundary traces are not bulk measures and are not set to zero by this argument.
   The positive witness source in (42.7) is not proved to be small in m2 L∞ (dt). Nor are all
physical-reference records shown to meet the original nongrazing comparison-pool constraints.
The original first-order single/cross-flight clearance, low-load incidence source, degenerate/critical
charts, section cuts and singular pairing terms therefore remain in (0.2). The present theorem
supplies a proved geometric mechanism for obtaining a physical reference without the previous
inverse-incidence clearance loss. A normalized source/pressure or original-capacity argument
must still exploit that mechanism, with the original full sums and order of limits, to close the
raw theorem.

          43. Summable physical coercivity and critical singular closure
   The maximum estimate in Theorem 41.3 is inherited. Summing its fourth power would
introduce a factor m + 1. We instead sum the positive gap before estimating the transverse
work. This gives one budget for the complete record, including arbitrarily small incidences.
Throughout, R ∈ [9/20, 47/100], m ≥ 1, and the notation Q, O, n, v, τ, Y, ℓ, δ, e, L, D, E is exactly
that of Section 41. Set
              r = 47/100,          N = 94,            a = 1/416640,         T = 187/25,       τ− = 3/50.          (43.1)
No probability, roof window or return event is changed by the following comparison.
Definition 43.1 (Closure of a fixed physical word). A closed physical reference is a coordinatewise
limit of actual regular records with the same finite ordered centers (O0 , . . . , Om ), with radii
converging inside the original closed radius interval. The record length and word are fixed while
taking this closure. At a limiting contact there may be zero incidence or an unlisted disk tangency.
We regard the limit as a polygonal reference, not as a definition of the billiard map at that
singularity, and do not assign it return labels by continuity.

Corollary 43.4 (Coercivity at a critical singular limit). If the closed physical reference has
normal endpoints, so E = 0, then for every Y ∈ K
                                                       m

Thus Q is the unique disk-product minimum,Peven if some internal incidences vanish. An

Proof. Here D = L, so G = A(Y ) − L. Apply (43.6). Nonnegativity and equality force every
coordinate to agree with Q; the approximate assertion follows from G ≤ η. In particular this
proof does not divide by a vanishing incidence, unlike the regular critical contact argument
alone.                                                                                    □
Corollary 43.5 (Unique fixed-word compactification). Fix m and its ordered centers. Any
sequence of actual regular normal-endpoint records of that word with Rj → R∗ in the original
interval converges to the same closed physical reference. Its roof equals ℓw (R∗ ). This asserts
uniqueness of the limiting polygon, not physical continuation, section-label continuity or a coarea
density at the singular limit.
Proof. The finite product of bounded disks is compact. Every convergent subsequence is a closed
physical reference with normal endpoints. By Corollary 43.4, each such limit is the unique
minimizer of the same functional at R∗ . All subsequential limits therefore agree, which proves
convergence of the entire sequence and the roof identity by continuity of Euclidean length. The
conclusion does not require the set of admissible radii to be connected. No orbit is inserted at a
radius where none is physical.                                                                  □
Remark 43.6 (What the singular statement does not remove). The limiting word may have
an unlisted tangency and a singular contact can carry paired boundary data not seen by bulk
collision measure. Uniqueness of its minimizing polygon does not bound the inverse roof Jacobian,
continue the return map, or show that its normalized paired source vanishes. All such trace
terms in the inherited source remain present. The constants above are intentionally nonoptimal;
their independence of the collision count and incidence is the conclusion.

Theorem 44.4 (Positive discrepancy payment on the unchanged roof). For every R, m, n, k and
nonnegative Borel weight h, let Π be the original exact event (1.1) and define, on the class CR,m
only,
                                                                 ∗

The pushforward is always by the actual original roof LR (Q), not the comparison roof. As an
inequality of positive measures,
                          Mh [Ncut ] ≤ Mh [Bρ ] + Kρ−4 Mh [ER ]           (ρ > 0).              (44.6)
It remains valid after a countable sum over all physical word cells and nonnegative marked copies,

114                                                       QIAN QI

Proof. Multiply (44.4) by the nonnegative actual weight 1CR,m 1Π h and integrate its product
with any nonnegative Borel test of LR (Q). This proves the measure inequality, first for bounded
weights and then by monotone convergence. Countable marked sums are handled by Tonelli
before any supremum or limiting operation. The conditional collision probability already contains
exactly the factor c−1
                    ∗ ; no second section normalization is introduced. There is no coordinate
change in this proof, and hence no unrecorded Jacobian or physical time multiplier. The density
statement is the order property of Radon–Nikodym derivatives when absolute continuity has
actually been established.                                                                     □
Remark 44.5 (The exact remaining normalization). The new term Bρ is a specified observation-
boundary occupation, and ER is the actual endpoint defect. Their occurrence is an explicit
error accounting for real labels, not a claim that either has small normalized density. The
class complementary to CR,m is not discarded. Equations (44.6) and (44.5) alone give no m−2
estimate, no high-s uniform decay, and no comparison-pool capacity. In particular choosing ρ as
a function of m requires estimates uniform at those widths; it is not justified by a fixed-width
result. The singular paired endpoint traces are not bulk pushforwards and are not declared paid
by this theorem. The complete source must still satisfy the original (15.8), with all exact labels,
roof intervals, ordered suprema and outermost B limit unchanged. This paper does not assert
completion of the raw pointwise theorem or its four-coordinate inversion consequence.

               45. Boundary localization of a global disk-word minimum
   The summable sublevel theorem gives a collision-independent budget, but does not by itself
locate its mass. Here global minimality pays the variational work in the interior by work in
two prescribed transition layers. A nonlinear recurrence then gives a uniform algebraic tail. No
regular physical continuation from the reference to the comparison is assumed.
   Let m ≥ 1 and let Q0 , . . . , Qm be an original regular physical record, or a fixed-word closed

         46. Transverse clearance and a uniform physical boundary layer
  Longitudinal displacement of a contact need not be charged as a loss of clearance: a sufficiently
narrow comparison strip confines that part of the comparison flight to its actual incident disks.
This gives a quadratic obstruction budget and a summable depth schedule. We keep the original
physical reference, disks, constants and notation of Section 45. Statements about arbitrary
sublevel points will be distinguished from those requiring a global minimum.
  For any feasible comparison polygon define
      βi = max{|vi⊥ · δi |, |vi⊥ · δi+1 |},      Γi (Y ) =        inf          dist(z, [Yi , Yi+1 ]) − R.   (46.1)
                                                             z∈Λ\{Oi ,Oi+1 }

Every third center is retained, including a center visited elsewhere in the same word. Put Γi (Q)
for the original closed flight.
Lemma 46.1 (A strip comparison with both incident disks retained). If 2βi < τ− , then
                                       Γi (Y ) ≥ min{Γi (Q), τ− } − βi .                                    (46.2)
The statement holds for arbitrary feasible comparison endpoints.
Proof. Use coordinates in which the original endpoints are (0, 0) and (τi , 0). Intersect each of its
two incident closed disks with the strip |y| ≤ βi . The longitudinal projections of these compact
convex sets are disjoint intervals: otherwise two points, one in each disk, would have the same
longitudinal coordinate and distance at most 2βi < τ− , contradicting disk separation. The first
interval precedes the second, since they contain respectively 0 and τi > 0. Thus the comparison
segment has strictly increasing longitudinal coordinate and stays in the strip.
   The portion projected into [0, τi ] is within βi of the original closed segment. If a left extension
exists, let Z be its intersection with the line of longitudinal coordinate zero. The segment from
Yi to Z is within βi of the segment from Yi to Qi , using the same convex parameter. The latter
segment lies in the first incident disk. The right extension is treated identically with the second
disk and Qi+1 . Every third disk is at distance at least τ− from each incident disk. Its distance
from a point of the comparison segment is therefore at least Γi (Q) − βi on the central portion,
and at least τ− − βi on either extension. Taking the infimum over the full segment and every
third disk proves the result. The argument includes endpoints, changes of the closest center,
and changes of the minimizing foot. It does not replace a closed flight by its infinite supporting
line.                                                                                                 □

Corollary 46.6 (A true central comparison subrecord). If D ≥ D0 , m > 2D, and the original
regular record is outside BD,R,m , then every global minimum has the actual regular first-hit
subrecord YD , . . . , Ym−D in the original Lorentz gas. The omitted end portions are not asserted
physical.
Proof. Every included contact is active, so the one-coordinate minimum condition places it on its
original circle with the exterior specular law and strictly positive incidence. Each included flight
therefore stays outside its two incident disks; strict positive clearance excludes every third disk.
These facts prove genuine first-hit behavior on each successive central flight. The implications
are simultaneous in the global minimum.                                                           □
   The source theorem controls total mass for bounded positive weights. It does not control
normalized roof-density peaks, arbitrary optical Jacobians, unbounded marked multiplicity, or
singular pairing traces. Its deterministic clearance mechanism and exact source limit do not alter
the original section, exponential guard or microscopic window.

Remark 47.4. The full source event is still Πn,k,m,R and its roof still Lm,R (Q). Neither the
central occupation nor the phase-corrected central length is substituted into that event. The
end portions, the section collar and the physicality-exception source remain present in the full
problem until their own required estimates have been proved. In particular Tclock,d alone cannot
bound |Ld (Y ) − Ld (Q)|: the term Wd is indispensable.

               48. Exponential confinement by physical anchor layers
   The polynomial estimates above do not exhaust the variational mechanism. The physical
turning bound supplies contacts at which the endpoint work of a whole comparison interval
can be paid by energy in its removed boundary layer. This yields a strict contraction after
a fixed number of actual collisions. The resulting exponential estimate concerns every global
disk-product minimum, including nonphysical and inactive ones. It is not obtained by integrating
a local endpoint derivative along an assumed physical continuation.
   Keep the original reference, comparison, radius and constants of Sections 45 and 46. In
particular Y is a global minimum of the unchanged disk-word problem, and the reference is
actual regular or a fixed-word closed physical reference. To distinguish it from the depth of an
index, denote the anchor threshold by a0 = 1/416640. Thus N = 94, r = 47/100, τ− = 3/50, and
                           r                          κ     3263680
                   κ=          = 3263680,    qa =         =          ∈ (0, 1).            (48.1)
                         a0 τ−                      1+κ     3263681
The symbol qa is a deterministic contraction constant; it does not replace the original guard
constant q0 = 47/53.
Lemma 48.1 (Common active geometry of global minima). For a fixed word and radius, all
global disk-product minima have the same ordered unit edge directions and the same active index
set. At every active index their vertices agree exactly. Both endpoints are active. Any difference
between minima lies along the common straight direction in a chain of inactive contacts. This is
a convexity fact about the comparison problem, not a new billiard map or a claim that its full
minimum is unique.
Proof. Let Y, Z be two minima. Their midpoint lies in the same product of disks and, by
convexity of A, is another minimum. The sum of the nonnegative defects in the flightwise triangle
inequalities is zero, so each such inequality is an equality. Both vectors of each edge are nonzero,
by the fixed consecutive-disk separation. Equality in the Euclidean triangle inequality therefore
makes them positive parallel vectors, and their unit directions agree.
   The Cartesian gradients at every coordinate consequently agree for all minima. If this common
gradient gi is nonzero, the minimum condition on its disk gives the unique point Oi − Rgi /|gi |.
If it is zero, its neighboring directions agree; this index is inactive for every minimum. At an
endpoint the gradient is a unit vector up to sign, so it is always active. On a consecutive chain of
inactive indices the edge direction is constant, and the differences between corresponding edges
of Y and Z are multiples of that direction. Starting at the preceding common active vertex and
summing these differences shows that all possible vertex differences on the chain are longitudinal.
No continuation through an inactive vertex or through an unlisted disk is inferred.               □

reference contacts are anchors, cu , cv ≥ a0 , then

Remark 48.6 (Rate, original guard, and probability topology). The contraction qa is close to one
because the explicit physical anchor bound is conservative. It is nevertheless fixed before any
collision limit, and its exponential decay is uniform on the entire original geometric family. It
                                                                              d/4
does not identify these rates with the much narrower original guard widths εq0 . In particular a
substitution of one depth schedule for the other would change the claimed microscopic problem
and is not made. Exponential geometric cost, exact original-source mass, inherited-pressure
conditional roof height, and the entire normalized shortage remain distinct assertions. None is
inferred from a finite trajectory computation.

        49. Comparison costs on the original roof and their normalization
   A deterministic small comparison cost and the density of the exceptional physical source are
different quantities. Here positive ordering pays the former on the unchanged physical roof. The
unnormalized roof-height input is stated explicitly; none of the geometric theorems just proved
depends on it.
   Fix R, m, n, k and d ≥ 2N , 2d < m. On any Borel class Pd of original records with a physical
central minimum as in Section 47, choose one such global minimum per physical disk word. At
fixed radius there are only countably many finite words. A chosen minimum is constant on its
word, so this choice gives Borel comparison coordinates on their physical domains. Alternatively,
by Lemma 48.1, the active central vertices of all such minima coincide. No joint measurability in
the radius is needed for the original order supR ess supt .

on the fixed labels. With Π exactly as in (1.1), define the positive measures

once. Finally dm ≥ θm and m /dm ≤ θ /m. All constants were fixed before the collision limsup.
Restricting a density norm to a roof interval cannot enlarge it.                               □
Corollary 49.2 (The dominated persistent low-load interior source). For γ ≥ 1, the same
positive-cost and conditional normalized estimates apply to every Borel restriction of the inherited
persistent low-load interior coefficient which is dominated by the original uncut roof measure as
in Lemma 12.1. This includes its original low-load selectors, all bounded by one. It does not
assert the corresponding domination for singular paired endpoint traces or for an arbitrary full
endpoint coefficient.
Proof. The inherited inequality is 0 ≤ cε,γ          ε
                                             per ≤ f (u, ϑ) ≤ A(u, ϑ) on each actual physical roof
segment. Multiplication by its nonnegative low-load selector and by f preserves the inequality.
Sum the actual source coefficients at their original roof and use the positive original-measure
comparison of that lemma. The result is a measure dominated by the right side of (49.2); the
preceding proof applies verbatim. This argument invokes that specific domination theorem and
its existing physical multiplicities, not an assertion that a boundary trace has a bulk density. □
Corollary 49.3 (Central observation costs with a stated frequency range). On the same source,
after retaining eisWd in (47.7), the nonnegative error majorant is
                           |s|Tclock,d + 2| sin(ζ/2)| Bρ,d + ρ−4 Tphase,d .
                                                                         

For fixed ρ > 0, the two comparison-cost terms have collision-normalized original-roof height
tending to zero when d = dm and |s| = o(m), under the stated pressure input, uniformly in the
two lattice twists and the return twist. The positive source weighted by Bρ,d remains an explicit
separate term.
Proof. Combine the exact twist estimate with the section payment and Theorem 49.1. The
lattice phase has modulus one, and 2| sin(ζ/2)| ≤ 2 for all ζ. The clock coefficient cost is bounded
after multiplication by m2 by a constant times |s|/m. At fixed ρ the other comparison term is
O(m−1 ). No estimate of Mh,d [Bρ,d ] was proved by this argument, so it remains on its own side
of the inequality.                                                                                □
Remark 49.4 (The exact scope of the normalization). The estimates above are for specified small
geometric costs on a proved physical central comparison class. They do not pay the indicator of
its complementary source, the original shallow end portions, the singular one-sided or paired
128                                            QIAN QI

boundary traces, a physical multiplier not dominated by the declared source, or a shortage of
roofwise comparison capacity. Nor does an estimate uniform on an increasing bounded frequency
range by itself justify the full unbounded Fourier integral. In particular the measure contraction
in Theorem 46.5 alone is not an m2 L∞ source estimate. All these distinctions persist when the
sharper exponential costs below replace the algebraic bounds.

Remark 50.5 (The retained order of limits and the remaining full source). Theorem 50.3 proves
limD→∞ supm,R,n,k,h ∥µobsD,R,m,n,k,h ∥TV = 0 on the entire original family. Corollary 50.4 proves a
stronger roof topology for specified exponentially small costs on its complement, under a precisely
named inherited input. Total variation of the exceptional source is not its roof essential height.
In particular these two statements do not imply that its m2 L∞ height vanishes.
   The original ε(B), hB , rB , γB , KB , microscopic roof windows and guard q0 remain fixed before
the collision limsup, and B remains outermost in (0.2). The positive shallow source, all required
physical multipliers, critical and singular paired traces, roofwise pool capacity, full conditioning
denominator and the complete spectral/arithmetic inversion errors still require their actual
original-source bounds. The exponentially accurate central comparison does not discard the
remaining end portions or change the full event. The final raw pointwise local limit theorem and
four-coordinate inversion are therefore not claimed complete.

        51. Physical tube volume and a normalized positive-source payment
    The total-variation estimate of Theorem 50.3 does not by itself bound a physical-roof density.
This section proves a comparison which does: an entire physical momentum disk can be spread
from each contributing critical tube, with its exact optical weight bounded below as well as
above. Distinct tubes are disjoint as physical sources. Invariance then pays their complete
volume before the original-roof coarea is performed. The result is uniform in collision count and
retains arbitrary Borel exact-record selectors. It concerns the guarded critical-tube union already
constructed in Section 34; no coverage of its complement is assumed.

Remark 51.6 (Exact scope and the still required full-source estimate). The new estimate pays
actual bulk source at its common physical roof through a proved lower optical volume and
physical tube disjointness. Its dependence on the fixed tube radius is explicit: ρ−2
                                                                                  ε . It establishes
no uniform bound as that radius tends to zero and therefore cannot be extended to every regular
trajectory merely by exhausting smaller guard classes. Neither singular paired traces nor a
full Im /|∇F | insertion is covered by (51.11). The original shallow and uncovered boundary
sources, comparison capacity, full physical multipliers, and the remaining spectral, arithmetic
and conditioning interfaces retain their exact obligations. In particular the original ordered limit
(15.8), the raw pointwise local limit theorem, and the four-coordinate inversion are not claimed
closed here.

        52. A finite-overlap physical atlas and original-roof source height
   We prove a height estimate for the complete class having a fixed margin in the original incidence
and clearance tests. The class includes both critical and noncritical physical trajectories. It
is not restricted to trajectories that continue to a critical orbit. The argument uses actual
two-dimensional families of trajectories and bounds their overlap before summing their positive
optical weights. Neither a whole-cylinder pressure estimate nor a mixing theorem is used.
                1/4

qR < 53/100 and q 7→ 2q/(1 + q 2 ) is increasing on (0, 1), so the largest section momentum is
less than 10600/12809 + 1/200 < 5/6. The other three types of section rectangle have smaller
momentum bounds. No section-distance margin is imposed in (52.1).

Lemma 52.1 (A common momentum neighborhood of every guarded record). For x ∈ Gε,m,R
put qx = (α0 , αm ) in local endpoint lifts and ζx = (p0 , pm ). The actual endpoint ball |q − qx |2 < aε
is physical with the same lifted word, and all its incidences and clearances satisfy the half-margins
(33.5). Its endpoint incidences exceed 1/2. Every |ζ − ζx | < 4ρ has a unique actual inverse on
that ball, with
                                 |q(ζ) − qx |2 ≤ R+ |ζ − ζx |/g < aε /2.                            (52.3)
Here and below uniqueness also holds among all regular physical trajectories of the same lifted
word with the given ζ.
Proof. On the closed ball, |h0 | + |hm | ≤ 2aε = δ∗ ε/8 < δ∗ ε. Theorem 33.3 gives the actual
trajectories and all half-margins, on a neighborhood of this closed ball. Its signed tangential
estimate bounds each endpoint momentum change     √ by Cinc δ∗ ε/8 ≤ ε/16 ≤ 1/64. Thus |p0 |, |pm | ≤
5/6 + 1/64 = 163/192 < 17/20 and c0 , cm ≥ 111/20 > 1/2. The original second variation
therefore gives G = Dq2 F ⪰ gI.
   For the desired momentum pair minimize F (q) − RDζ · (q − qx ) on the closed ball, where
D = diag(−1, 1). At its boundary, putting h = q − qx and using ∇F (qx ) = RDζx , the outward
radial derivative is at least
                                 ga2ε − R+ |ζ − ζx |aε > ga2ε /2 > 0.
A boundary minimizer is impossible. Strong convexity gives one interior minimizer, where
∇F = RDζ. The inverse function theorem and strong monotonicity give its smooth dependence
and (52.3). This minimization takes place inside a ball already proved physical; it does not assert
that an arbitrary stationary continuation is physical. Finally, with equal endpoint momenta the
left side of the support inequality (10.3) is zero. Strict positivity of every physical incidence
forces all contacts to coincide. This proves the asserted global wordwise uniqueness, even across
disconnected original event components.                                                             □

union covers Gε,m,R . Each physical initial point is in at most 25 of the tubes, counting all words
and grid points. Writing u(x) = |∂α0 pm (x)|−1 , one has throughout a tube
                   −1                                                       ρ2

The Euclidean distance is at most ρ/ 2 < ρ. For every nonempty pair (w, z) choose one such

inverse gives the specified tube and center. The local parametrization is injective as a physical
source map: its momenta determine the point by wordwise uniqueness. Every other assigned
source point with the same pair (w, z) agrees with the constructed inverse at its own momentum
138                                           QIAN QI

by that same global uniqueness. Thus the family covers the entire class, not just the chosen
representatives. Different constructions for a pair define exactly the same physical inverse on the
disk.
   A physical initial point determines its unique complete lifted word. If it lies in Tw,z , its
momentum is within 2ρ of z. Each coordinate interval of length 4ρ contains at most five points
of ρZ. There are consequently at most 25 possible grid centers. This multiplicity bound uses
the physical momentum map; it introduces neither independent marks nor a factor equal to the
number of collision words.
   In the chosen endpoint ball all tube points and the center have distance less than aε /2 from qx .

logarithmic derivative bound in Lemma 34.1, integrated in both directions, gives distortion at
most exp(D∗ aε /ε) ≤ exp(D∗ δ∗ /2) = Cdist . In momentum coordinates the unconditioned invariant
collision probability is u dζ/(4π). The disk area is 4πρ2 . Integrating the lower distortion bound
proves the volume estimate in (52.4).

using si ≤ 2σ di . The tangential derivative obeys the identical incidence bound: differentiate

of Proposition 29.2. Also |Dαi | ≤ J∗ si ∥η∥∞ and J∗ ≤ Cc . This proves (52.7) in the circular
coordinate metric. The clearance estimate is for the complete closed flight segment and every
third disk; nearest-disk switches and disks visited at other times are already included in its
Lipschitz proof.                                                                                   □

its predecessor copy is also preserved.

Lemma A.2 (Physical-time turning and the 94-flight anchor, inherited). Every actual regular
trajectory segment of physical duration H has total interior incidence cosine at least γ = 1/4480.
Every actual free flight has length at most H, and every complete 94-flight block has an interior
collision with ci ≥ 1/416640.
Proof. A collision changes the unit velocity by a vector of length 2ci . Hence for a physical
segment q(s), 0 ≤ s ≤ H, with initial outgoing velocity v, direct integration gives
                                                            X
                                 |q(s) − q(0) − sv| ≤ 2H             ci .
                                                           0<ti <H
Endpoint collisions do not affect this estimate: an initial collision is accounted for by the outgoing
v, and the velocity change at H does not affect positions up to H. If the sum were less than
1/4480, the deviation would be less than 1/400. Apply Lemma A.1 to the initial straight reference
segment and evaluate at its interior perpendicular foot. The actual position would then have
                          COLLISION RECORDS AND RAW LOCAL INVERSION                                149

distance less than 179/400 + 1/400 = 9/20 ≤ R from a lattice center, contradicting exterior
nonpenetration. This proves the incidence sum.
   A straight free flight of length exceeding H would contain an H segment intersecting a disk
interior by the same covering lemma, a contradiction. Consecutive distinct disks are separated
by τ− = 3/50, so 94 flights take at least 94τ− = 141/25 > H. Apply the physical-time bound to
their first H units. The relevant collisions are among the 93 interior collisions of the block. Thus
their total cosine is at least 1/4480, and one is at least 1/(4480 · 93) = 1/416640. All collisions
are actual; no probabilistic independence, symbolic model or number of words is used.             □

            Appendix B. An exact flux identity for unvisited near misses
   The incidence load does not see a trajectory whose original guard is witnessed only by
clearance. We introduce a counting observable on the same physical flights, not a new collision
map. Throughout this section let
                  g∗ = 1 − 2R+ = 3/50,               δ∗ = g∗ /4 = 3/200,     0 < δ ≤ δ∗ .
The symbol δ∗ here is a one-flight geometric constant, not the outer microscopic width δ(B). All
disks still have radius R.
   For a regular outgoing collision x, write its flight as q(x) + tv(x), 0 ≤ t ≤ τ (x). Let Zδ (x) be
the set of lattice centers other than the two incident centers for which
                   0<     min       |q(x) + tv(x) − z| − R < δ,        Nδ (x) = |Zδ (x)|.
                        0≤t≤τ (x)

This counts near misses, not collisions. In particular neither the collision index, the physical
roof, nor the section-visit count is incremented by Nδ . A center is counted once on a flight.
Tangencies may be assigned value zero on their collision-area null set; no assertion about a
positive pairing-endpoint trace follows from this convention.
Lemma B.1 (An interior foot and uniform endpoint separation). Every center counted by Nδ
has its perpendicular foot in the open physical flight. If tz is the time of that foot, then
                                      tz ≥ g∗ − δ,       τ − tz ≥ g∗ − δ.                        (B.1)
The candidate centers, after translating the initial incident center to zero, belong to the finite set
                                    {z ∈ Λ \ {0} : |z| ≤ τ+ + 2R+ + δ∗ }.
Write N∗ for its cardinality. This constant is independent of the itinerary length and of all exact
record labels.
Proof. At either incident point, the distance to a third center is at least 1−R, since distinct lattice
centers have distance at least one. Its distance to the third disk is therefore at least 1 − 2R ≥ g∗ .
A minimum below δ ≤ g∗ /4 cannot occur at an endpoint. The nearest point on a line segment
is then its perpendicular foot, whose distance from z is at most R + δ. By the reverse triangle
inequality its distance along the line from either endpoint is at least (1 − R) − (R + δ) ≥ g∗ − δ.
Finally a foot is at distance at most R + τ+ from the initial center. Adding its distance from z
gives the finite-set bound. This proof uses the actual segment, including both of its endpoints,
rather than an infinite-line substitute for the clearance event.                                     □
   Put vϑ = (cos ϑ, sin ϑ) and vϑ⊥ = (− sin ϑ, cos ϑ). For a mark z define the signed line coordinate
b = (z − q) · vϑ⊥ . Translate the marked center to zero. For σ ∈ {−1, 1} and 0 < u < δ∗ the
oriented line with b = σ(R + u) has closest point −σ(R + u)vϑ⊥ . Follow this line backwards to its
last actual collision. Reduce that collision by a lattice translation to the initial fundamental disk,
and denote the resulting outgoing state and marked-center displacement by
                                        XR (ϑ, σ, u),      ZR (ϑ, σ, u).
150                                           QIAN QI

These maps use straight free flight until an actual disk is met. There is no reflection at the
closest point. The finite-horizon assumption ensures actual collisions in both directions, apart
from null singular parameters on which these maps can be defined arbitrarily. The interval from
the preceding collision to the following one remains the original physical flight.

   There is also a global multiplicity assertion to prove. A marked physical flight maps to its

In fact the disk of radius R + δ∗ around the marked center does not intersect any other actual
disk, since 2R + δ∗ < 1. The central disk is missed because the line has impact magnitude greater
than R. Following the line in both directions therefore gives a physical flight whose incident
disks are different from the marked disk. Its foot is interior and its clearance from that disk is u.
This is inverse to the marked-flight map, modulo the prescribed lattice translation. Multiple
near misses on one flight give different marked copies; they are neither lost nor identified.
   For measure-theoretic precision, partition the line parameters by the actual backward incident
disk and the forward incident disk, and exclude their tangent boundaries. These partitions
are Borel; on each regular piece the disk-intersection formulas and their inverses are smooth.
Tangency boundaries satisfy the line equation |bz | = R. For any fixed alternative center their
equation in (ϑ, u) is a graph in u (with one of the two fixed signs), hence has planar Lebesgue
measure zero. There are countably many centers. The corresponding singular collision sets are
also null by (B.4) on regular charts. Exhaust these charts by relatively compact subcharts and
disjointize them. Applying the local Jacobian formula to bounded functions on finite unions,
then using monotone convergence for both chart unions and a ∧ n, proves (B.2) without a prior
integrability assumption. Setting a = 1 gives 2(2π)δ/(4πR) = δ/R.                                  □
Corollary B.3 (All physical depths with the original normalization). For arbitrary 0 < δj ≤ δ∗ ,
