         Appendix C. Clearance strips transverse to physical test curves
  A measure bound alone gives no short-curve control. The positive separation between an
unvisited disk and the incident disks supplies a transverse derivative which remains nonzero even
as an incident angle approaches grazing. We specify the time orientation explicitly: forward
near misses are tested on increasing-slope curves, and past-flight near misses on decreasing-slope
curves.
  Use physical arclength s = Rα increasing counterclockwise and p = sin φ. Fix a finite U > 0
and put
                              κ− = 1/R+ ,        κ+ = 1/R− ,
                               D0 = κ+ + U + (1 − R+ )−1 ,
                                                                                              (C.1)
                                   ℓ0 = π/(2D0 ),    a0 = κ− (g∗ − δ∗ ),
                                           p
                             Cgeo = 2N∗ 1 + U 2 /a0 .
All lengths below are Euclidean arclength in (s, φ), not configuration-space arclength. A test
is a connected graph over an s interval, of length at most ℓ0 . Let Eδ+ be the forward physical
near-miss event {Nδ ≥ 1}, and let Eδ− be the analogous event on the preceding actual flight.
Theorem C.1 (A grazing-uniform, one-flight strip bound). For every C 1 test graph W + with
0 ≤ dφ/ds ≤ U , and every C 1 test graph W − with −U ≤ dφ/ds ≤ 0,
                          |W + ∩ Eδ+ | ≤ Cgeo δ,         |W − ∩ Eδ− | ≤ Cgeo δ.               (C.2)
The bound is uniform in R, in the homogeneity strip of the test, and in its distance to the grazing
boundary. Arbitrary Borel restrictions, including exact record restrictions, can be added to the
sets on the left. Regular pieces are understood with their physical multiplicity one.
Proof. On W + the direction angle is ϑ = s/R + φ(s). For a fixed candidate center z ̸= 0 set
                             tz = (z − q) · vϑ ,     bz = (z − q) · vϑ⊥ .
Differentiation in the increasing s coordinate gives the signed identity
                           b′z = − cos φ − tz ϑ′ ,       κ− ≤ ϑ′ ≤ κ+ + U.                    (C.3)
At a physical near miss, Lemma B.1 yields tz ≥ g∗ − δ∗ . Hence −b′z ≥ a0 there. It is the tz ϑ′
term, not a lower bound on cos φ, which prevents degeneration.
152                                              QIAN QI

    We must not multiply this estimate by an uncontrolled number of components. Choose a
continuous argument β(s) = arg(z − q(s)) on the graph. Since |z − q| ≥ 1 − R+ , one has
|β ′ | ≤ (1 − R+ )−1 . Moreover
                                  (β − ϑ)′ ≤ (1 − R+ )−1 − κ− < 0,
because R+ < 1/2. Its total variation is at most D0 |W + | ≤ π/2. Thus the set tz > 0, equivalently
cos(β − ϑ) > 0, is at most one interval: two distinct positive-cosine intervals are separated by a
negative interval of angular length π. On the interval tz > 0, formula (C.3) says that bz strictly
decreases. Each of the two impact bands (R, R + δ) and (−R − δ, −R) therefore has multiplicity
at most one in this coordinate. Restricting to the portions which are actual near misses can
break them into pieces, but cannot increase that multiplicity. By the one-dimensional change of
variables on this monotone map,
                              Z
                                                                 ds ≤ 2δ/a0 .
                                {s: z is a physical near miss}
            √
Since dℓ ≤ 1 + U 2 ds, summing over the finite candidate set from Lemma B.1 proves the first
bound. This sum counts only centers in one physical flight. It does not count iterated collision
words or homogeneous continuity components.
   For the second bound, the collision time-reversal involution is I(s, φ) = (s, −φ). The outgoing
velocity at Ix reverses the incoming velocity at x. Consequently its next straight flight is the
reversal of the previous actual flight at x. This proves
                                        Eδ− = IEδ+ = TR Eδ+                                  (C.4)
on the regular collision space.    The involution is an isometry in (s, φ) and sends W − to an
increasing-slope graph. Apply the first bound. Borel restrictions decrease lengths. Taking
monotone limits of truncated regular test pieces gives the same bound at grazing limits; an
exactly tangent parameter contributes zero transverse length by the strict derivative above.
This last statement does not delete any paired-source boundary trace in the older coarea
construction.                                                                             □

explicitly here. It is not the stronger target-roof local limit estimate.

space, not the essential height of a roof density. In particular, the absence of frequency growth
in this absolute estimate is not decay as |ξt | → ∞ and does not justify integration over that
unbounded frequency. Such decay, or another rigorously justified pointwise bridge, is still required
for the original raw target.

           Appendix E. Exact-record disintegration at an unvisited mark
  The flux coordinates also give a direct representation of the remaining roof problem which does
not discard the exact return, displacement or collision information. We state it as an equality of
measures first, because a conditional angular roof kernel need not have a density without proof.
  For 0 ≤ j < m define the initial state

lift; translating the marked disk to zero changes none of these relative displacements. For almost
every u, define the positive Borel measure on the original roof line

E.2. Compatibility with the complete original source. All identities through (15.3) and

order of limits. The new results treat the mechanism which is invisible when Jε = ∅: genuine
unvisited-disk near misses. They do not pay low-severity shallow incidence without clearance,
prove abundance of the full nongrazing comparison pool, or settle positive critical-entrance and
section-cut pairing traces.

theorem is retained as a finite-count theorem, not presented as another missing payment for that
already restricted source. The term “low” in all inherited source symbols means low incidence
load only. In the monotone exhaustion of Proposition 10.4, every marked weight is explicitly
assigned zero on the omitted collision-area null set; its extended integral is taken only over the
stated regular charts. This convention never sets a positive one-sided pairing trace to zero.

              Appendix F. Scope of the retained comparison statements
   For clarity, we record the precise scope of the earlier allocation statements. None of their
algebraic maximality assertions is used as a substitute for the physical estimate in Theorem 7.1.
Proposition F.1 (Fixed-window scalar maximality and old-family dominance). Fix a Borel
comparison subspace, 0 < r < ∆, and K > 0. Consider nonnegative measurable weights w+ , w−
with w+ + w− ≤ 1, supported on the respective one-sided window conditions in (6.2). Among
scalar accepted heights Z(t) satisfying
                                                            Z
                           0 ≤ Z(t) ≤ U (t),     Z(t) ≤ K       (w+ + w− )p∨ dθ,

the greatest is Vr,K (t) = min{U (t), KPr (t)}. The allocation is global at the roof and does not
couple individual source sheets to comparison sheets.
   For a compatible old fixed family with window width rold , physical overlap N and block cost κ,
the explicit conditions
                            0 < r ≤ rold ,     ℓcomparison < ∆,      K = Nκ
imply V old (t) ≤ V   r,N κ (t) on the same original source and comparison space.

Proof. The support and one-copy conditions give w+ + w− ≤ Qr . Hence every Z is bounded by
the stated minimum. On Pr > 0 choose w± = Vr,K q± /(KPr ); use zero weights otherwise. This
is a measurable attaining certificate. The separate source multiplier is βr,K defined in Section 7.
For the old family, retain only active source blocks at t. Their comparison supports lie in the
r-pool by the displayed width condition. Summing the blockwise budget and using physical
overlap at most N gives V old ≤ N κPr ; disjoint original source blocks also give V old ≤ U . The
minimum proves the assertion, with countable families obtained by monotone convergence on
their common roof versions.                                                                     □
Proposition F.2 (Strictness relative to roof-independent whole-interval blocks). In the interval-
source model, the roofwise certificate class is strictly larger than the old class of roof-independent
angular source blocks that requires a comparison interval to contain the translate of the entire
                         COLLISION RECORDS AND RAW LOCAL INVERSION                                 157

source interval by [0, r] or [−r, 0]. This statement compares certificate classes and does not assert
a Lorentz realization.
Proof. Let s = 1/1000 and r = 1/10. On two disjoint angular sets of measure one give both
endpoint traces value one. Let their physical intervals be (0, s) and (3s/8, r + 7s/8), and select
only the first as source. Take ∆ > r + s/2 and K = 1. For 3s/8 < t < 7s/8 the second interval
contains [t, t + r], so Pr (t) ≥ U (t) = 1. Neither physical interval has length r + s, which is
necessary for the old translated-whole-source inclusion. Angular subdivision does not alter these
intervals. Thus the old class cannot allocate a nonzero whole-source block while the roofwise
class accepts the source on the indicated roof interval.                                        □
F.1. Limit order and the remaining estimate. The constructions in this article are evaluated
first at finite m, R, n, k with B, rB , hB , KB already fixed. Positive physical sums are completed
before taking the essential roof supremum, the radius and exact-label suprema, and the collision
limsup. Only then is B sent to infinity. The assertion that every positive-length segment contains
a sufficiently small window at a fixed count supplies no uniform modulus for this order.
   The remaining estimate is precisely
                 lim lim sup sup m2                       Umicro         ng      
                                              ess sup      B,m (t) − KB PrB ,m (t) + = 0.
                B→∞ m→∞ R,n,k           t∈Cm,R,n,k (M )

No inference to this estimate is made from bounded wordwise optical weights, local integrability, a
finite diagnostic, or a positive finite-count incidence. Theorem 7.1 proves the entire visited-grazing
payment in this order; all remaining positive classes in the displayed expression are still part of
the original pointwise problem.

constant depends on the number of marks, not on their locations. No word-count factor has been
suppressed, and no pressure conclusion is drawn by summing its right side.
Lemma G.3 (One-sided limits at simultaneous physical mechanisms). Fix a positive-length
pairing segment and approach one of its ends from the regular interior. The collision points,
velocities, flight lengths, visited incidences and third-disk clearances have limits along the entire
approach, even if several of the following occur simultaneously: visited grazing, unvisited tangency,
a section boundary and a critical incoming endpoint. The limiting reduced Hessian and coarea
factor are those in (5.3) and (5.4). No continuation of the exact event through its boundary is
asserted.
                          COLLISION RECORDS AND RAW LOCAL INVERSION                                159

Proof. Lemma 5.1 gives limits for q and z along the entire segment, not only a subsequence.
Hence the initial collision point and p0 = −z cos θ/R converge. The section cap gives the outgoing
normal component c0 > 0 at the limit, so the initial velocity converges as well.
  Suppose inductively that the departure point x and unit velocity v of a designated flight
converge. For its designated next disk with centre O, the entry time is
                                             q
                       τ = −(x − O) · v −      ((x − O) · v)2 − |x − O|2 + R2 .
This is the entry root on the actual regular itinerary. Its radicand is nonnegative along the
approach and has a nonnegative limit. The root is continuous also when the radicand tends to
zero; τ ≥ τ− > 0 prevents a zero-length flight. The arrival point therefore converges. Reflection
by v 7→ v − 2(v · n)n is continuous even at tangency, so the next velocity converges. Induction
applies a finite number of times. An earlier disk may become tangent only at the limiting flight;
this does not alter the designated entry-root limit taken from the original event.
   At fixed itinerary and radius, the bounded flight lengths restrict all possibly nearby third-disk
centres to a finite set. Distance from a compact line segment to each such disk is continuous in
the endpoints. Their finite minimum, and hence the original clearance, has a limit. A section
decision may change at the limiting state, but its coordinate limit is still obtained from the same
side. None of these continuity arguments requires the boundary mechanisms to occur separately.
Formula (5.3), with Z −1 uniformly bounded, then gives the Hessian limit even with several
vanishing incidences. The fixed-direction coarea formula gives the asserted limit for χ.          □
Corollary G.4 (An explicit uniform trace modulus). A permissible constant in (5.6) is
                                                        √
                                                500Creg 2Λ◦
                                         CH =                  .                                  (G.4)
                                                   4πc∗ λ2◦
It is common to all original sheets, collision counts, radii and exact labels. Its inherited dependence
is precisely the regular variation constant Creg ; the remaining constants are the fixed geometry
and section constants displayed in this article.
Proof. Corollary 4.4 and χ ≥ λ◦ give AIm ≤ 500ϱ(m−2)+ /(4πc∗ λ◦ ). Integrate the regular
derivative bound between u and v, and apply Lemma 5.1 to that subsegment:
                                   Z v               q
                                         z −1 ds ≤ λ−1
                                                    ◦  2Λ◦ (v − u).
                                    u
Multiply the constants and pass to the already proved endpoints.                                    □
   Any nonempty collection of visited endpoint grazings belongs to Γ, and one vanishing marked
incidence is already sufficient for its zero source statement. Proposition G.2 additionally controls
their simultaneous product. This does not alter the distinct treatment of an unvisited third-disk
tangency.

            Appendix H. Dependency, reference and normalization audit
   The direct clearance proofs use the original positive disk separation, the uniform finite-horizon
hypothesis, circular curvature, elastic reflection and elementary change of variables. The arbitrary-
time stable-test estimate additionally uses Input D.1. Neither uses uniform pressure, a twisted
spectral gap, or the unproved localized-shortage estimate to prove its own premise.
160                                            QIAN QI

H.1. The precise imported curve estimate. In the inspected 10 October 2013 author version
of Demers–Zhang [6], Lemma 3.2(b), printed page 14, bounds the sum of suprema of the forward
arclength Jacobians on homogeneous inverse-image pieces, uniformly for W and for maps in the
stipulated class. The geometric-family statement is Theorem 2.7 in that version; the varying-
arclength qualification is Remark 2.9(b). The inherited module 241’s numbering must therefore
be matched to a specified version rather than silently treated as verified. Its stronger finite-testing
and pressure argument is not needed for Theorem D.2.
   For this paper the coordinates, Euclidean arclength Jacobian, short-curve curvature bound,
homogeneous partition, fixed subdivision length, and map-family constants must all agree with
Input D.1. Changing boundary perimeter with R requires the coordinate transport and its
uniform equivalence; changing the subdivision threshold changes Cg by a bounded factor. These
interfaces remain explicit retained hypotheses here, rather than a claim of an independent
specialist reconstruction.
   In the inspected 19 September 2022 author version of Baladi–Demers [7], Lemma 2.2, printed
pages 7–8, is a distortion statement across stable curves in the same homogeneous backward cell.
Its closure convention excludes true singularity curves. It is not a four-record, roof-conditioned
m−2 estimate. The present proofs make no such inference.
H.2. Checks which enter the proofs. The radius factor in (B.4) is R, not R2 or 1/R. The
two impact signs and the full direction circle give the factor 4πδ before collision normalization.
Exactly one section factor c−1∗ occurs in (E.2). The foot has physical time coordinate tz , so the
sign in (C.3) is a minus for both the boundary motion and the direction motion. Increasing-slope
curves, not forward stable curves, are used for the forward event.
   The uniform lower bounds used above are strictly positive on the whole closed radius interval:
               g∗ − δ∗ = 9/200,       κ− − (1 − R+ )−1 = 600/2491,         a0 = 9/94.
The occurrence of N∗ in the curve estimate, but not in the exact flux identity, is deliberate: the
former is a one-flight union bound on a test curve, whereas the latter is a global one-copy change
of section. Empty future blocks are included by l = 0. The denominator 1 − r1−a is finite only
for a < 1; no estimate at the endpoint a = 1 is claimed.
   The source identities in the retained proof bodies are unchanged. All positive sums are
completed before essential suprema and the collision limsup. Taking B → ∞ in a stable-test
estimate would not by itself permit taking that limit in the target-roof norm. Fixed-m simulations
and symbolic identities check algebra and implementation only; they do not certify either the
inherited uniform dynamical inputs or the remaining raw pointwise theorem.
H.3. What is retained, and what has not been reconstructed. The complete active
revision-112 manuscript and its ten active core proof bodies are retained in this manuscript, and
an unmodified copy of the downloaded active packet is supplied in provenance/v112-active.
The original repository additionally contains the recursive revision-111 archive, with source
tree 790e627efe3a89a4549123d7e9acacf24089c287. That recursive archive was not exported
by the completed CI artifact available for this revision. Its identity is preserved; it has not
been represented as a locally reconstructed, independently audited set of 267 historical proofs.
The included source manifest and preservation audit distinguish byte-verified local preservation
from remote tree pinning. In particular the historical spectral, arithmetic, induced-return and
four-coordinate inverse-problem inputs are not replaced by the new clearance lemmas.

 Appendix I. Critical-roof and fold audit: normalization and ordered closure
   The new identities have different kinds of uniformity. The clearance-to-endpoint determinant
is an identity for each finite physical itinerary and every marked flight. The fold and finite-type
                         COLLISION RECORDS AND RAW LOCAL INVERSION                                 161

bounds are uniform over Borel selectors on a specified chart, with specified derivative constants.
The critical coarea error is uniform over selectors but depends on the chart’s third-derivative and
weight-modulus constants. The critical coefficient sum is over all regular physical words at fixed
collision count, relative to the retained uniform pressure input; it is unscaled. None of these
qualifiers can be replaced by another.
I.1. Two distinct uses of reversibility. At a normal endpoint, the actual reflected ray
reverses its incoming velocity. This proves the reversing-periodic-orbit assertion without adding
a reflection at an unvisited mark. In collision coordinates, time reversal also gives the derivative
identity M = JB −1 JB. The first assertion concerns actual points and their section tests; the
second concerns the tangent map on the regular domain. At an unvisited tangency, only the
one-sided reduced action coefficient is used. A smooth extension of that action does not define
the physical map through the forbidden side.
   Even at a regular reversing orbit the original section need not be reversal invariant. The
occupation identity is A2m = Am + AIm when both original endpoints are in the section, not
automatically 2n. The coefficient J describes nearby collision area pushed to the roof. It is not a
conditional law on a measure-zero periodic orbit.
I.2. The exact small-scale error and its range. The radius r0 in Theorem 17.3 is a local
endpoint-chart radius. The height increment is s = t − tx , and its allowed range is 0 < s < r02 /3.
Neither r0 nor s replaces the original microscopic observation window. In particular the explicit
error constant                             √
                                        2π 3{2Lw + Kw(0)}
has not been shown summable after multiplying the full physical source by m2 . Decreasing r0
separately for each orbit does not establish that summability. A general Borel selector retains
the exact fraction aE (s); only a verified finite tangent-sector structure justifies a limiting angular
fraction.
   The local fold exponent 1/2 is produced by integrating a square-root conditional singularity
across a transverse clearance direction. A higher finite type has its stated exponent 1/ℓ and
derivative budget. Full critical roofs have a different two-dimensional local coarea law; the
fold theorem is not applied there when its clearance transversality fails. A countable disjoint
source-chart covering preserves positivity and exact multiplicity, but does not itself bound the
sum of the chart constants.
I.3. The unchanged closure statement. All original positive coefficients and the complete
nongrazing pool are still those of Section 15. The remaining statement is (15.8), with the
full physical sum first, then the exact-label and radius suprema and the original roof essential
supremum, then the collision limsup, and the outer limit last. A successful continuation must
control clearance and critical charts, positive one-sided pairing traces, the low-incidence source,
and the actual available comparison capacity in that topology. The new calculations neither
subtract these unpaid classes nor introduce a new name for the shortage.
   The accompanying programs check the signed generating derivative, stability determinant,
normalization, fold constants, selector error and actual horizontal period-two physical rays.
The latter are not asserted to lie in the large-collision central roof window. Exact rational
checks of their optical Schur complements and finite ray-derivative comparisons test independent
implementations; they are not substitutes for the written continuous proofs or the unresolved
uniform dynamical estimates.
162                                               QIAN QI

           Appendix J. Cross-flight normalization and source-scope audit
  The physical order used in Section 19 is
                                     a       ℓ         ℓh−1       b
                 first mark foot −−→ q1 −−1→ · · · −−−−→ qh −−→ second mark foot.
Only q1 , . . . , qh are reflections. The incident collision before the first foot and the incident collision
after the second foot are also present in the original full trajectory. Neither is deleted from the
record; neither auxiliary marked circle changes the clock.
   The finite-horizon quantity τ+ denotes the fixed uniform bound on the length of every real
free flight in the original radius family, as specified in the retained model. This revision does not
certify a new numerical value for it. All new constants display that dependency. In particular,
with
                               Lpair = τ+ + 97/100,        Lend = τ+ + 191/200,
Npair and N◦ are the numbers of nonzero lattice vectors of length at most Lpair and Lend ,
respectively. For either L, they can be evaluated exactly as
                             #{(a, b) ∈ Z2 \ {(0, 0)} : a2 + ab + b2 ≤ L2 }.
A conservative bound, without any enumeration assumption, is ⌊(2L + 1)2 ⌋ − 1: the disjoint
open radius-1/2 disks around these centers and the origin lie in the radius-(L + 1/2) disk; area
comparison proves the claim. This is an end-candidate count, never a bound on the number of
full physical itineraries.
   The two-mark same-flight    identity counts ordered distinct centers. For chronological pairs the
expansion N (N − 1) = j Nj (Nj − 1) + 2 j<k Nj Nk accounts explicitly for both orders. A
                           P                    P

repeated center at two times still supplies two chronological marks. The exact physical prefactor
1/(4πR) precedes every clearance substitution, while the original section restriction contributes
1/c∗ only when passing to νR  ∗ . Neither the factor two nor the section normalization is absorbed

into a supposed independent probability.
   In the inherited identity Eδ− = IEδ+ = TR Eδ+ , both expressions on the right denote set images
on the regular invertible collision space. Thus 1E − (x) = 1E + (TR−1 x) almost everywhere; the
                                                          δ           δ
displayed image is not TR−1 Eδ+ . The null-set convention is a bulk-measure convention, not a rule
that positive one-sided endpoint traces vanish. Similarly, the critical coarea density in Section 17
is the contribution of its stated coordinate ball and selector. It is not automatically the complete
global density near that roof value.
   The new proof chain has three distinct levels. The foot standoff, Jacobi identity, tangential-end
uniqueness, exact coarea and stable metric comparison are proved directly in Sections 19–20. The
uniform all-separation density and complete factorial bounds additionally require the explicitly
retained unconditioned physical pressure inequality supR,l Pl (R) ≤ Q∗ . The raw pointwise
theorem still requires (15.8). No conclusion at the second level is substituted for the third. The
native historical proof bodies 260–276 are preserved, and modules 277–278 alone are the new
numbered proof modules in this author revision.

          Appendix K. Angular-source dependency, metric and limit audit
   The new geometric source formulas use only lattice separation, actual circle reflection, the
inherited physical finite-horizon  bound and the exact one-mark parametrization. The direct
source norm in (21.8) is C ∥ρC ∥∞ , not a sum of masses and not a global collision-space L∞
                          P

norm. It contains both signed first marks. The second mark also has two signs, already paid in
Ct ; no additional sign or root factor is to be appended to H∗ . The one-mark normalization is
inserted only after the root sum, as 1/(4πR). The original source normalization appears once, as
c−1
 ∗ , inside a positive exact-event domination.
                             COLLISION RECORDS AND RAW LOCAL INVERSION                                          163

External curve growth and physical coordinates. The inspected author version of [6] dates
from 10 October 2013. Its Lemma 3.2(b) is the homogeneous stable curve-Jacobian sum; its
proof specifies (H2)–(H4). Theorem 2.7 states uniform properties for its geometric family, and
Remark 2.9(b) discusses a small change of boundary arclength. We use none of these statements
as an exact-label roof local limit theorem or as a sum over all physical cylinders. Input 22.1
states exactly the forward counterpart needed here; source-curve membership and a common
curvature allowance are essential parts of its invocation. Theorem 21.1 verifies a concrete bound
K0 for that membership check. It does not independently re-prove expansion and distortion for
the admissible class.
   Time reversal is I(s, φ) = (s, −φ), an isometry of the original collision-coordinate arclength,
and ITR I = TR−1 on the regular set. Thus forward pieces of an unstable curve correspond to
backward pieces of its reflected stable curve, with equal arclength Jacobians. This conversion
does not reverse the original measured return record in a theorem statement; it only verifies the
curve estimate.
   For a local common boundary coordinate at reference radius R0 , write AR (s, φ) = (R0 s/R, φ).
If mA ≤ |AR v|/|v| and |AR v|/|v| ≤ MA , then conjugating a curve map gives
                                                                        |AR vl |/|vl |
                               J common TRl (x) = J physical TRl (x)                   .
                                                                       |AR v0 |/|v0 |
There is only an endpoint ratio, at most MA /mA , not a factor (MA /mA )l . A compact finite
local cover of [R− , R+ ] gives common constants whenever the cited geometric curve hypotheses
are verified in those local charts. This is a coordinate comparison, not an affine deformation of
the triangular physical lattice. The input is kept explicit rather than asserting that compactness
alone verifies (H2)–(H4) or the larger pressure and pairing chain.
Boundary and quantifier conventions. All regular first-hit intervals are genuinely physical.
Future singular cuts are exhausted through the one-copy curve partition. This removes zero
angular or curve measure in bulk integrals only. It says nothing about the positive one-sided
trace of a paired source at a singular endpoint. Those traces and critical ball selectors remain as
in the inherited optical proofs. Likewise the set-image convention is unchanged: IEδ+ and TR Eδ+
mean images on the regular set, whereas TR−1 (E) denotes its preimage; inherited equalities are
used with their specified null-set convention.
   The curve-growth supremum ranges over all stated radii, all nonnegative iterate counts and
the entire admissible curve class. The new kernel holds for every fixed positive first clearance
and all positive integer separations. The root sum is countable and positive before any essential
supremum. These facts do not commute a roof supremum with an integral over first clearance.
The original central roof window and arithmetic labels remain fixed. The collision limsup is taken
before the outer B limit. The explicit remaining proof obligation is (15.8), including first-order
clearance, critical and boundary sums, incidence-only source and original nongrazing capacity.
None is assigned zero mass or zero height merely because the angular-source theorem has been
proved.
